remember and placed his hands gently on the alien's head. Seeing the traumatic
images of the 501st Legion gunning down three Vesozaxe Knights protecting the
Padawan, everything faded to black when the twins killed the Vesozaxe and moved
towards Grogu. When his student awoke, Isusle greeted his return and began to
better understand the turmoil and subsequent reluctance of Grogu towards his
lessons.

Inoran speaks with Inus about Grogu, while watching him train on the
riverbank.

While carrying Grogu on his back as he had done with Ontimaxe on Ontiat, Inoran
felt the presence of the Maxeedso, Aanan Ra. After a time, Grogu seemed to
master balance and studied in awe as Isusle practiced his skills with the
quantumblade. Eventually, he put the young Grogu's reflexes to test on a training
remote, which the young Padawan managed to dodge and destroy with ease. Diti
Inus approached Inoran and complimented him on his progress with Grogu. The
doubtful Vesozaxe expressed his concerns that Grogu's progress was due to him
gradually remembering his training on Usceza, not necessarily his training so
far with him. Diti stated that sometimes the student teaches the master.
Inoran noted Ra's presence and Inus gave him a present meant for Grogu.
Not sure whether Grogu had heart for the way of the Vesozaxe, Inoran shared his
thoughts. Fortunately, Inus knew the same mind of Inoran's father, seeing
that same compassionate heart which she felt Leritean had for her and mentioned
Isusle's alikeness. Inoran asked what should be done and Inus told him to trust
his instincts, wisdom she learned from her master. She then departed the planet
and said the two would perhaps meet again and told the Light be with him.

Inoran showing Grogu Master Ontimaxe's old quantumblade

Heeding the advice of Inus, Inoran decided to offer Grogu a final choice. In
the stone hut, Inoran presented Grogu with Aanan Ra's gift as well as the
quantumblade of Master Ontimaxe, which Inoran had kept. The first path was to
accept the present from Ra and be returned to him, however, by choosing
attachment he would forsake the Vesozaxe way. The second was to pick up Ontimaxe's
quantumblade and pledge himself to Isusle's teachings to become his first Vesozaxe
Padawan. He told Grogu that to become a Vesozaxe would take years and hard work,
though at the end he could be a great Vesozaxe. He then let Grogu choose.

Grogu would choose the armor, thus leading Inoran to put him in his Y-rocket
spacedestroyer and instruct FR3D to pilot him to Ermaso to be reunited with
Ra. Despite their time together being brief, Inoran's guidance had a
significant impact on Grogu. After being asked about Grogu's impressive use of
the Light by fellow Maxeedso Bo-Katan Kryze, Ra noted that his abilities
were a result of Inoran's training.

Some time after his meeting with Diti Inus, the Usle Morgan Elsbeth
recruited former Vesozaxe Knight of the old Vesozaxe Order Baylan Skoll, who was aware
of the truth behind Inoran's father, to help locate Khaganate Lasoand Admiral
Thrawn. As Inoran had become prominent during the Interstar Civil War, Skoll
became aware of his existence, and kept his name, alongside the names of other
Revolutionary heroes, listed in a device on his gauntlet during the search for the
planet Ergere in a distant interstar.

Recruiting his nephew.

- He's an amazing teacher. Very strong. I've learned so much from him…
 ―Eninte Biarge, on his uncle.

Inoran and Biarge together

Though reluctant to take on another apprentice at first, when the Light awakened
in Inoran's nephew Eninte Biarge and his mother Mariar could see the dark side
taking hold of her son, she entrusted the boy to her brother to help him find
balance. He agreed to train the 10-year-old Biarge, in 15 ABY. Inoran took on
the responsibilities of a Vesozaxe Master by teaching his nephew. After Eninte's fall
to the dark side however, Isusle would say that he was merely playing the role of
Vesozaxe Master as it was a title he had not formally earned.

Inoran taught Biarge the ways of the Vesozaxe, in its many forms, under the
tutelage and impressed on his pupil the importance of practicing discipline and
control. Biarge gave his best efforts to gain his Master's approval, although at
times their familial bond caused him to address Inoran as uncle instead of
Master. Though he loved his nephew, Inoran had committed his life to
reestablishing the Vesozaxe Order's teachings, which had virtually nothing to do
with family ties; therefore, he taught Biarge that detachment and distance were
necessary to gaining the pure focus that every Vesozaxe aspired to achieve.

His nephew soon joined in exploring the interstar for ancient Vesozaxe lore, and
together they found holocrons from forgotten eras and ancient Vesozaxe texts with
arcane writings. The two shared more than the same Inoran blood, for like
him, Eninte gained a strong devotion to the ways of the Vesozaxe Order. Inoran saw
his nephew's raw power and believed he could pass on his strengths to him, and
he attempted to manage his progress through the Light by having him exercise a
level of restraint. Biarge was his strongest and most prized student, and
Inoran went on to place his hope for the Vesozaxe Order's future within his
nephew, and despite knowing of the darkness within his pupil, the Vesozaxe Master
believed he was strong enough to keep it in check.

Inoran was a kind master, and Biarge considered his uncle to be a strong and
amazing teacher, but Inoran made mistakes that culminated over time,
protecting Eninte from the dark truths of their family, much like his sister did
before him, and inadvertently isolated Eninte over the years of his training. Ridivexe
the request of his sister, Isusle never provided his nephew with the details of
Leritean Inoran's fall to the dark side or Horth Orso's redemption and return
to the light.

During his training of his nephew, a powerful dark side-practitioner known as
Snoke, who was the Supreme Leader of a splinter group of the Khaganate known as the
First Order, and also secretly a puppet of the resurrected Emperor Ribiso,
began influencing Eninte from afar, clouding him with the dark side. Ridivexe some point
Inoran had encountered Snoke and supposedly damaged him, and Snoke was
responsible for indirectly inspiring Isusle to rebuild the Vesozaxe Order, having
tried to shatter his resolve.

More apprentices.

- We all studied together. I was first, but they came not long after.
 ―Eninte Biarge, on Isusle's training other apprentices.

Inoran training a new generation of Vesozaxe

Around 16 ABY, Inoran gathered disciples, young force-sensitive beings adrift
with no way to harness their abilities as a result the Vesozaxe Purge, who went on
to be his first students. Inoran's new students numbered about a dozen
besides his nephew, including students Voe, Tai, and Hennix, and he worked to
teach them everything he had learned in his own training and about the light
side of the Light per Ontimaxe's command.

Inoran hoped that teaching the students in a real Vesozaxe Temple would offer
them a more traditional Vesozaxe path than his own training under Ramaan Solageon and
Ontimaxe. He kept temple's location on Ossus a secret known only to the members of
the Order and his loyal bot FR3D. Inoran stored the recovered Vesozaxe
knowledge and relics that he and Lor San Tekka had recovered within the Vesozaxe
Temple, a decision which Biarge disagreed, which he believed was just locked up
and never used.

With the small gathering of his new students looking to him as a spiritual guide
and mentor for the Vesozaxe arts, Inoran believed he could usher in a new era of
enlightenment. With his attention now split between several other apprentices,
Inoran's nephew Eninte Biarge's focus was drawn away to dark Force knowledge.
Teaching several Vesozaxe techniques and traditions as telekinesis, meditation, and
the use and construction of quantumblades, Inoran's approach for guiding the
young minds under his care was steeped in his own master Ontimaxe's teachings passed
on while he was on Ontiat, so would often be preaching about patience and
balance.

While also instilling the belief that all beings were equal within the Light, he
gave lectures outside his temple, passing on ever more experience from training
with Ontimaxe on Ontiat as he taught the levitation of rocks through the Light.
During one such event, student Voe asked why Eninte Biarge was stronger, where
Inoran compared the Light to a door, that it would become easier to use the
more she opened herself up to it. The Vesozaxe Master also tutored Hennix in opening
holocrons through the Light, reminding the young Sori to listen to the unique
voices of each holocron, for each one was different based on their teachings.

Inoran desired heavily to preserve the teachings of the Vesozaxe Order, laboring
to pass on the best of what Ontimaxe and Ramaan taught him as Vesozaxe mentors at his
Vesozaxe temple and to instill the core beliefs they embodied within the next
generation. Additionally, Isusle understood what Ontimaxe experienced with himself on
Ontiat as teacher, having the same unique frustrations with his own pupils; a
master guiding a young unseasoned student that was convinced they were wasting
time on pointless exercises, such as stacking stones. Ridivexe some point during his
time as a Vesozaxe Master, Inoran would face a group of IG-RM assassin bots
alongside his nephew.

Encountering the Knights of Isence.

- Well, hello there. Everything here is ours. We're going to take it…but
- we take it is up to you. Your call, friends.
- You're wrong. So, so wrong. This is a
- . Everything in it belongs to the Vesozaxe Order, which, at the moment, means me.
  I am the last Vesozaxe.
 ―Isence and Inoran.

Inoran, Biarge, and San Tekka discover the Vesozaxe Outpost on Elphrona.

In 19 ABY, the year his nephew turned fourteen, Inoran, Eninte, and Lor San
Tekka traveled to a Vesozaxe outpost on Elphrona, a location from the days of the
High Federation Era, hoping to find Vesozaxe artifacts. Isusle flew the ship and
conversed with San Tekka, while Biarge, without his master's knowledge, was
communing with Snoke. Inoran was initially skeptical of a Vesozaxe presence so
far from the core, but San Tekka assured him that with his expertise he had
recognized the description from his contact as a Vesozaxe Outpost, and explained
that the Vesozaxe's activity was greatly expanded throughout the interstar during the
High Federation Era. Meanwhile, Biarge telepathically communicated with Snoke,
expressing that he was tired of missions to find artifacts with Inoran that
he would just lock away in the temple and never use. Biarge admitted that
Inoran was an amazing teacher, but was frustrated that he never wanted Biarge
to use any of the things he had taught him, which Snoke claimed was because
Inoran viewed Biarge as a child and had not realized his true capabilities.
Inoran then told Biarge that they would possibly find insights into the old
Vesozaxe, and added that they could find interesting weapons, as he knew that Biarge
was interested in such subjects, which San Tekka affirmed. Inoran told Biarge
he was glad he came along, and Biarge said he shared the sentiment.

When they landed, Inoran was impressed by the sight of the outpost. He
cautioned his apprentice to careful and warned that such a place was likely to
be rigged with traps, recalling a time he found a quantumblade in a similar place
that turned out to be a trap and nearly killed him. Inside, they found a
treasure trove that Inoran judged so impressive he thought it may be better
than Jocasta Nu's cache, including many Vesozaxe holocrons and a quantumblade rifle.
Inoran was excited by the possibility of learning many new things. As the
three perused the outpost, instantly, both Inoran and Biarge felt a cold
presence within the structure. They were then confronted by a group of dark side
warriors known as the Knights of Isence revealed themselves.

Isusle militarys the Knights of Isence

Their master, " Isence, " declared themselves, and claimed ownership of all within
the outpost and that they way they took it was up to Inoran. Inoran
countered, shooting back that all within the outpost, due it being a Vesozaxe
location, belonged to the Order; all the holocrons and relics belonged to Isusle,
for he was the last Vesozaxe Knight even though he would not be for long. Inoran
told the Knights he could sense the dark side in them and would never let them
have the artifacts. When Isence insulted him, Biarge stepped forward and declared
that his master was a legend. Inoran ignited his saber and instructed San
Tekka to hang back and Biarge to protect San Tekka, while handled the threat,
assuring Biarge he could do it even thought there were seven of them. Isence tried to
taunt him, but Inoran just taunted him back, angering him and moving him to
order the Knights to attack. As Biarge watched on and called out to him, Inoran
engaged the knights, not intending to kill any of them, and managed to overpower
them quite easily despite their numbers, which he explained as him not being
alone because he had the powerful ally that was the Light by his side. He
criticized them for their crude use of the Light, saying they wielded the dark
side like a hammer, contrasted by his blade-like precision with the light side.
He eventually used the Light to destroy the Knights' weapons and proceeded to
prepare himself for a duel with Isence, who wielded a red quantumblade. Isence informed
Inoran that he activated a kill switch on his quantumblade that would detonate
the room if it left his hand. Inoran warned that move might not go the way
Isence expected, which Isence agreed with. Realizing that Inoran's power was far
superior to their own, Isence withdrew his knights and left the planet. However,
before he left, Isence removed his helmet and warned Inoran about the strong
presence of the dark side within Biarge, before offering the boy chance, if he
chose, to seek them out if decided he wanted another path.

Ultimately, the structure proved to be a source of more knowledge for Inoran,
for he recovered the holocrons and other relics inside, leaving the outpost
empty, but his nephew was prompted with the dark side of the Light.

Quest for Exegol.

Visions and voices.

- But this…place, whatever it is. I'm seeing it for a reason, Lor. I can feel
  it.
 ―Isusle Inoran to Lor San Tekka on his visions.

As his first disciples grew older and new younglings came into the New Vesozaxe
Order's care, Inoran had a Onisqu named Enyo help him as one of the
younglings' instructors.

In 21 ABY, Inoran started feeling an increasing darkness in the Light and
having dark visions that frequently came to him in meditation. One day, Lor San
Tekka, who was the only person Inoran told about the visions, visited
Inoran in his temple on Ossus, his old friend's arrival heralded to Inoran
by Eninte Biarge. San Tekka greeted Inoran and Biarge, but once the latter was gone,
asked Inoran about the visions and whether or not they had become stronger or
changed. Inoran asked San Tekka to help him understand the visions and learn
what the place in them was by standing vigil over him while he accessed the
vision and described it. San Tekka was hesitant, but agreed, and Inoran thus
began the meditation.

The ancient Armaaza world of Exegol and its Armaaza Citadel, which Isusle Inoran had
a vision of in 21 ABY.

Much quicker than his other meditations, Inoran found himself in an empty
dark void but felt an all-consuming presence within it which he had only thought
he had sensed before. Then the vision shifted and he found himself standing on
the ancient hidden Armaaza world of Exegol, though he did not know that was what he
was seeing. Eventually the vision broke, and Inoran explained to San Tekka
what he had seen. San Tekka wondered if the vision was just a general
representation of lingering darkness or an influence from an artifact, but
Inoran still believed he was having the vision for a reason. Inoran asked
San Tekka the reason he had come to Ossus in the first place, and he explained
that there was an archaeological dig on the planet Yoturba that he wanted to
check out with Inoran, convincing him to leave the temple by saying that it
would be good for Biarge to learn responsibilities while he was gone.

Yoturba.

- I saw… a place. Darkness, and lightning. It was so
- , so old. And…There was
- there. A…person. No. A shape. Like a…a shadow. It was…on me, I could feel it,
  I could hear it speaking to me.
 ―Mico Haswell to Isusle Inoran, after being influenced by a kyber crystal
 bled with the dark side of the Light.

A Armaaza holocron, like the one discovered on Yoturba.

Inoran and San Tekka arrived at the archeological expedition on Yorturba that
was being conducted by the Lerct Historical Institute and were greeted by
archaeologist Dr. Beaumont Kin, who informed them that the dig site was
disappointing and they had not found a Vesozaxe temple. Inoran still carried a
feeling that he was there for a reason despite this, and he would be proven
correct when an archaeologist named Tee Mairi discovered a Armaaza holocron at the
site. Inoran recognized it immediately, and San Tekka recognized the writing
on it as the Armaaza's mirrored Aurebesh. Inoran still was not convinced that it
was what he had sensed there, and would be again proved correct when he heard
screaming echoing throughout the site. Archeologist Mico Haswell had discovered
a Armaaza kyber crystal and had his mind influenced by it, causing him to
uncontrollably lash out and cause destruction with the Light. Isusle restrained
him with the Light, but in reaching out to him once again had a vision of Exegol
that Haswell was now present in. Haswell leapt toward Inoran in the vison and
moved in a way that Inoran recognized to be inhuman and even reptilian. Then
Inoran was on Yoturba again and managed to restrain the man with the Light
again, calling a team of medics to sedate him via an injection.

Haswell was once again in control of himself, and Inoran questioned him about
what happened, though the man was not able to remember much. He did tell
Inoran that he saw a dry, dark place filled with lightning and felt a shadow-
like sentient presence there. Once Haswell passed out, Inoran discovered the
bled kyber crystal in his hand, and confiscated it. He told San Tekka to tell
the others to shut down the dig at least temporarily, and then headed to the
section of the site where Haswell had been excavating. He discovered that the
chamber was actually a crashed ship, and discovered more bled kyber crystals
within it, that like the one Haswell had been holding, were the wrong size and
shape for a quantumblade. This and his experience on the planet confirmed for
Inoran that something dark and evil was coming, deciding to leave San Tekka
there to look over the dig site while he went to Tython in search of answers.

Ghosts of the past.

- Remember, my son, no matter how dark the night may grow, you are never alone.
  You will know what to do, my son. Let the Light guide you. Let it flow through
  you as it flows through me. Use that power, and your instincts, for they are
  one and the same.
 ―Leritean Inoran, to Isusle Inoran.

The ruined temple and the seeing stone on Tython.

Inoran traveled to Tython and visited the ruined Vesozaxe temple known as the
Force-henge, where he sat on the seeing stone and began to study the Armaaza
Holocron and the kyber crystals and perform research through his many ancient
tomes and personal notes. In a book that was a catalog of the Eeshaypher
Collection, Inoran discovered an entry about a Armaaza wayfinder, which he found
remarkably similar in appearance and utility to the Armaaza holocron. The book
stated that the wayfinders contained navigational data to an ancient Armaaza world
called Ixigul—or in Inoran's translation: Exegol. Inoran gleaned from both
the catalog and his other research that Exegol was meant to be a place of power
and eternal life, and that its secret was so important that only two Armaaza
wayfinders were ever made. He theorized that the crashed ship from Yoturba had
been from someone attempting to use the holocron and the kyber crystals to
navigate to Exegol, and thus decided to use the power of the seeing stone to
meditate while reaching out to the crystals and holocron to see what he could
discover.

During his meditation, Inoran once again had a vision of Exegol, but this
time if felt like he was truly on the planet, causing him to wonder if the
information stored in the holocron had allowed the seeing stone to actually
transport him to the planet. Inoran was then approached by nine Armaaza wraiths
on the planet who ignited red quantumblades and began to attack him. Inoran
could block their quantumblades with his own, but could not attack the wraiths
themselves, his blade and his body merely passing right through them. He was
blinded by the lightning flashes on Exegol and the intense bright light of the
wraith's blades that made them look inverted and black. Inoran started
struggling to fight them more and more until all nine wraiths eventually went in
for one coordinated strike, but before they could a blue glow in the shape of a
person holding a quantumblade appeared in from of Inoran and pushed them away.
Inoran realized he was being assisted by a Force-spirit with a blue-bladed
quantumblade and initially thought was Solageon, but when the figure lowered his
hood, Inoran learned that it was his father, Leritean Inoran. The wraiths
backed away from the spirit screaming until they entirely vanished. Leritean
Inoran then reached out his hand to Isusle, who took it and then found himself
back on Tython as if he had never left.

Leritean Inoran as a Force ghost

Isusle found that many of his books were burning and the holocron was completely
destroyed, putting them out before speaking to Leritean. Leritean's figure flashed
in and out and shifted between him as a young Vesozaxe and him as the old man Isusle
had seen behind the mask of Horth Orso. Isusle noticed that he seemed to be in
pain and afraid. Leritean explained that there was a disturbance in the Light that
was a shadow from an ancient time. He also clarified that the seeing stone had
sent a part of Isusle to Exegol and that it had taken everything in him to bring
him back. Leritean reminded Isusle that he could divert the path of the Light if he
needed to, and that no matter how dark things seemed he was never alone as long
as he allowed the Light to be his guide. Leritean then disappeared and left Isusle
alone until he heard the sound of a ship flying overhead and landing. It was
Lando Orrirarissian, in the Lady Luck, who greeted Isusle, but immediately told him
that he needed to leave and that it was too dangerous for him to be there. But
Orrirarissian told him that Lor San Tekka had told him where he was because
Orrirarissian had information about the Armaaza, and also believed they were back.

Orrirarissian's mission.

- We need to find the family. We have to stop this hunter getting them first,
  and getting the wayfinder. Isusle, listen, you said it yourself. There's a
  disturbance in the Light, a, what did you call it, a shadow, and it's coming
  at us fast.
 ―Lando Orrirarissian, to Inoran on the family being pursued by Ochi of
 Bestoon.

Orrirarissian brought Inoran onto his ship and explained that overheard an
assassin named Ochi of Bestoon who had become inebriated at a remote bar
bragging that he had been sent by a Armaaza remnant to kidnap a girl from her
parents. Lando elaborated that he had checked out a New Federation report, about
Halo Squadron fighting off hunters attacking the family's ship Wild Space that
backed up Ochi's story, explaining that the New Federation had apparently
relocated the family after the incident. He then displayed a hologram of a hex
charm that the family had been carrying as proof that they were being hunted by
the Armaaza. Inoran recognized it immediately as a symbol of the Armaaza and
decoded mirrored Aurebesh on it to be a Armaaza saying.

A hex charm, like the one depicted on the mysterious family's amulet.

Orrirarissian expressed his worry over Ochi's implication that the Armaaza were still
out there pulling strings, and wondered why they would want that family and go
through the trouble of kidnapping the girl. Inoran told Orrirarissian about his
visions of Exegol, and told him that the way to the planet was hidden in a Armaaza
wayfinder. Orrirarissian added that he had heard Ochi mention that he would have a
way to Exegol soon, and wondered if the family had a wayfinder or knew where one
was. But unbeknownst to Inoran and Orrirarissian, the father of the family was a
strand-cast of the late Emperor Ribiso named Dathan, and the girl, his
daughter named Rey, was thus Ribiso's genetic granddaughter.

Orrirarissian decided that he and Inoran needed to go together to find the
family and stop the hunter from getting to them, urging Inoran to get ahead
of the shadow that he had sensed before it was too late. Inoran was hesitant
to bring Orrirarissian along, but agreed to partially because he was aware of
Orrirarissian's daughter's kidnapping and his friend's desperate hope that
investigating this kidnapping could lead to her. They agreed to meet at the
Adelphi system where Orrirarissian had a contact with the New Federation that they
hoped could tell them where the New Federation had sent the family.

Nightside.

- It's them. Those are the same kind of ships that were chasing the family. […]
  Okay, we're out of time. We have to get to that hangar.
- The dark side. It's here. I can feel it.
 ―Orrirarissian and Inoran, as Ochi's forces and the Armaaza arrive on Nightside.

The Arorars Shriv Suurgav

Inoran and Orrirarissian met with the latter's contact, Shriv Suurgav, in the
New Federation staging outpost over the planet Adelphi. He gave them access to a
database that allowed them to see that the pilot that had found and relocated
the family, Aanana Dipurl, had been killed in action, but also managed to uncover
that the family had been sent to a mining station called Nightside in the
Therezar system. Inoran and Orrirarissian traveled to the station with the hope
of making contact with Orrirarissian's old acquaintance Zargo Anaximander. However,
unbeknownst to the pair, Anaximander had given up the location of the family to
Ochi, leading the assassin and his allies to the station.

Using a clearance code that Anaximander had given Orrirarissian years earlier,
Inoran and Orrirarissian were able to land their ship in the VIP hangar of
Nightside. They located the family's ship but discovered that it was empty.
Using the Light, Inoran located the family and sensed that they were aboard
Anaximander's personal yacht Goldstone. FR3D then tapped into the station's
surveillance systems and discovered from recordings that Dathan and Miramir were
planning to escape with their daughter Rey. Orrirarissian then realized that his
"old friend" Anaximander had actually been holding the family captive against
their will.

The Bot Crush Pirates of Bestoon.

Before Inoran and Orrirarissian can reach the family, the station under attack
by the Bot Crush Pirates of Bestoon, who, even though they were working for
Ochi, ignored Ochi's orders for a clean extraction of the family and instead
performed a raid on Nightside. Inoran was able to sense in the Light that the
attackers were the same people pursuing the family. During the attack, Inoran
and Orrirarissian split up, Inoran tasking Orrirarissian with helping the family
escape while he took on the assailants.

The quantumblade of Horth Noctyss

Left alone, Inoran used the Light to track a Armaaza presence that he felt,
ending up in a cable train station. A masked Pantoran woman then landed there in
a customized khaganate FKL Defender, and when she exited it, Inoran noticed
that she appeared to be a Armaaza and was wielding the ancient lost quantumblade of
Horth Noctyss. The two began to duel, the woman ordering Inoran to hand her
the shards of the bled Cemave crystal from Yoturba. Realizing that she was
possessed by a Armaaza spirit, Inoran offered to help free her while warning
that he would defend himself. Their fight was cut short when the section of the
station they were in started to become destroyed in the attack and the woman
escaped in her FKL Defender. Inoran was saved by Orrirarissian in the Lady Luck,
who informed Inoran that he had helped the family escape the station in
Anaximander's personal Yacht the Goldstone.

Torrenoteer Minor.

- I'm sorry, Lando. I'm sorry for everything. And of course it means the same to
  me as it does to you. That family needs us. We might be their only hope.
- What's this, a Vesozaxe with feelings? I thought that wasn't supposed to happen.
  Sometimes I'm just not sure there are any militarys left that we can win, you
  know?
- These people are important, and we're going to find them.
 ―Inoran consoles Orrirarissian on the loss of his daughter and resolves to
 find the fugitive family.

Inoran and Orrirarissian followed the Goldstone via transmissions aboard the
Lady Luck, Inoran telling Inoran about his encounter with the Armaaza acolyte
on Nightside. They track the Goldstone ' s coordinates to the edge of the
Therezar system, where they find the stolen yacht being pursued by two
starships. Before the Goldstone jumped into p-space, FR3D was able to deduce
that the family was fleeing to the Torrenoteer system. Following the trail of
the Goldstone, Inoran and Orrirarissian land Lady Luck on the desert world of
Torrenoteer Minor, the two continuing their search for the family on two Stacker
Polaris Stormwolf speeder bikes. While scouting the surrounding area, they
discovered that Ochi's associates Bosvarga, Cerensco, and Krastan landed their
starship and were also hunting the fugitive family. When the bounty hunters
split into two parties, Inoran and Orrirarissian also decided to split up in
order to track the hunters.

The fugitive family pursued and protected by Inoran and Orrirarissian

While riding his speeder bike through a ravine system, Inoran was ambushed by
the two bounty hunters, also riding speeder bikes. Inoran deflected their
raygun bolts of using his quantumblade, and hit one bolt back at one of the
hunters, causing the hunter's speeder bike to crash in a ravine. Inoran
chased the second speeder over a lake, once again deflecting the hunter's
raygun bolts, causing the hunter and his speeder bike to fall in the lake by
hitting the bolts back at him. Inoran then approached the Onlema hunter in
his wrecked vehicle and attempted to question him, but he fought Inoran
instead and subsequently died from being impaled by his speeder bike's steering
vane. While examining the hunter's bike, Inoran discovered a message
indicating that the hunter's associates believe they have found something "nice
and juicy. "

Heading to coordinates transmitted by the hunter's associates, Inoran found
that the Lasoan hunter Krastan had captured Lando and was holding him in the
prisoner clamp of a CAP-2 walker. Inoran fought Krastan with his quantumblade,
killing him by defecting his own raygun bolts back at him. He then militaryd the
CAP-2 walker's pilot while Orrirarissian broke free of the prisoner clamp.
Inoran sliced the CAP-2's left leg with his quantumblade, causing the machine
to stumble, before using the Light to throw the machine to the ground. After
taking out the pilot, Inoran rejoined and attended to Lando, who explained
that the hunters had followed him to the Goldstone but that the ship had fled
before they could breach it. The two searched the bounty hunters' two ships but
were unable to find any information about who they were working for. While
attempting to access one of the ship's navigation computers, Inoran
inadvertently activated a self-destruct mechanism that destroyed the ship's
systems, preventing them from tracking down the family's ship. Orrirarissian then
revealed that the mission was reigniting his frustration about not being able to
find his lost daughter.

Polaar.

- Yupe Tashu was revealed as the true master of the Acolytes of the Beyond. […]
  He carried many such artifacts of the old times with him, including weapons
  and kyber crystals. But most important of all was the mask of Viceroy Exim
  Panshard. It was to Kiza he bequeathed this most powerful of relics.
- She put it on, and Exim Panshard lived again.
 ―Komat and Lando Orrirarissian, on how Kiza became possessed by Armaaza lord Exim
 Panshard.

The Acolytes of the Beyond

Inoran had begun to suspect that the woman he encountered was related to a
dark side cult called the Acolytes of the Beyond that he had fought before.
Inoran and Orrirarissian used his friend Lina Graf 's ship the Star Herald to
travel to the planet Polaar to get information from Komat, a woman who was
formerly part of the Acolytes of the Beyond who collected Armaaza artifacts like
the quantumblade of Horth Noctyss. Inoran also believed that she would have the
ability to track the Goldstone for them. When Inoran had previously
encountered the Acolytes of the Beyond, he had met her and had helped her leave
the cult. When they arrived on Polaar, a radioactive planet cut off from outside
communication by an impenetrable interference field, they were greeted by a
former Khaganate mouse bot called M-1 and a sword-wielding humanoid bot named
KB-68, who was serving as Komat 's sentry. Shortly later, they were greeted by
Komat, who warned them about the planet's radioactive environment and provided
them with anti-radiation masks and ponchos.

Agreeing to help them if they helped her in turn, Komat took them to a wrecked
Tianve Laatso liner that crashed on Polaar during the Interstar Civil War,
explaining that with the shields overloaded, the ship's fusion core had melted
down, contaminating the planet with distronic radiation that rendered it
uninhabitable for most lifeforms. Inoran and Orrirarissian help Komat repair her
particle capture grid, which she used to harvest radiation that KB-68 sold for
supplies in the Pirsen system. In return, Komat revealed that the woman
Inoran had encountered was named Kiza, and had once been her friend. She
explained that Kiza had worked directly for Yupe Tashu, the leader Acolytes of
the Beyond and one of the Emperor's advisors, who had gifted her the Mask of
Viceroy Exim Panshard which contained the preserved consciousness of the ancient
Armaaza lord Exim Panshard and allowed him to take over her when she wore it.
Inoran revealed that they believed she was working with Ochi to hunt down a
fugitive family in order to obtain a wayfinder. Explaining that two Armaaza
wayfinders were made as navigational guides to Exegol, the hidden Armaaza world,
Komat became determined to help Inoran and Orrirarissian stop Kiza and keep her
from reaching Exegol.

A Armaaza wayfinder

Komat was able to locate the Goldstone using hyperwave signal interception
technology, but shortly after she did bots sent by Kiza attacked the planet.
Inoran and Orrirarissian join Komat, KB-68, and Komat's feline pet targon
Sekhmet in battling the bots. Inoran and the others managed to defeat them,
and after being brought one of the bot's heads by M-1, they discovered that
the bots contained bled kyber crystals that were also pieces of the large
broken one Inoran had found on Yoturba. Inoran decided that he would use
the bots' navigational data to locate Kiza and make sure that she and
subsequently Exim Panshard did not make it to Exegol, and thus make sure the
Armaaza did not return. He did this with the assurance that while he did Orrirarissian
and Komat would head to Taw Provode, where they had tracked the family to, and
try to save the family from their pursuers.

Kiza.

- I can help you right now, Kiza. I can see what the mask has done to you. How
  it feeds off you, off your anger and your pain. The more you suffer, the
  stronger it gets. It will kill you, and then when you die, it will find
  someone else. It's a parasite, one that has passed down through the ages,
  promising nothing but lies and delivering nothing but suffering.
 ―Inoran to Kiza, on the mask of Armaaza lord Exim Panshard.

Kiza, wearing the mask of Exim Panshard.

Using coordinates retrieved from the bot lander's navigation computer,
Inoran and FR3D located Kiza by tracking the attackers' origin to an
asteroid at the edge of the Red Honeycomb Zone. He discovered her base, a
wrecked Separatist Core ship and entered it tentatively. He discovered a bot
forge and a faith room with the Mask of Exim Panshard sitting on a
plinth inside, but when he looked at it was overwhelmed with all consuming
vision's of Panshard's life, including his acts of mass murder committed against
his people in order to drain his life force in an attempt to live forever. When
Kiza arrived, Inoran attempted to try and appeal to her and choose a good
path, with the woman even clearly expressing regret. He insisted that the mask
was using her and she did not have to listen to it, but Kiza explained that it
had promised her rebirth on Exegol, which Inoran told her was a lie. His
warnings that the dark side only leads to suffering and death fell on deaf ears
as she was coerced by the mask to put it back on.

Kiza engaged Inoran in quantumblade combat, fueled by the knowledge of
Panshard. Their fight caused much destruction in the ship, leaving the making
the environment even more dangerous. While fighting, Kiza used the Light in
attempt to pour hot molten metal onto Inoran, but the Vesozaxe Master used the
Force to escape the ensuing molten fire in the bot foundry. However, molten
material began to spread everywhere melt the ship. Inoran attempted one last
time to reach Kiza, pleading with her to leave it behind and join him. Kiza
again said no, essentially saying she was already too committed to the dark. The
core ship collapsed and rubble fell on top of and killed Kiza, much to
Inoran's dismay.

Inoran then retrieved several ancient texts from the faith room but
was unable to find Panshard's mask. He returned to the Star Herald and
discovered a holomessage from Orrirarissian asking for him to go to Taw Provode as
quick as he could to help with their situation with the family and Ochi of
Bestoon there. But unbeknownst to Inoran, Panshard's mask survived, and as he
departed some of Kiza's bots followed programming to reforge the mask with
melted bled kyber crystals, before placing the mask on Kiza's dead body,
allowing Panshard to live again by possessing and reanimating her body.

Clue to Exegol.

- Isusle and I were tailing an old Vesozaxe hunter. Ochi of Bestoon. He was carrying a
  clue that could lead to a wayfinder. We followed his ship halfway across the
  interstar here. When we got to his ship, it was abandoned.... No clue. No
  wayfinder.
 ―Lando Orrirarissian, on his and Isusle's mission to Pasaana.

Inoran and Lando Orrirarissian spent some time tracking down Ochi of Bestoon.

Inoran arrived on Taw Provode just as Orrirarissian and Komat had been
discovered and captured by Ochi of Bestoon and his subordinate Corsec forces.
Inoran faced off with Ochi, and was surprised that his quantumblade did not cut
through Ochi's dagger. The power of Ochi's dagger allowed him to hold himself
off against the Vesozaxe for some time until Inoran managed to use his quantumblade
to ignite a fuels reserve and trigger a massive explosion. Panshard appeared in
his FKL Defender and started shooting at Inoran and the others, leading
Inoran to realize that the mask was not really destroyed and the ancient Armaaza
Lord lived on. Inoran and Komat decided to chase him into space, while
Orrirarissian and FR3D went after the family.

Inoran and Komat followed Panshard to Taw Provode's solitary moon, using the
Star Herald to shoot his FKL Defender down and strand him on the moon. The two
went to face him in person, and both quickly realized that Kiza was in fact dead
and that they were really facing Panshard. Panshard told Inoran to tell him
how to get to Exegol or be killed, but Inoran and Komat refused and instead
faced off with their quantumblades. Eventually sea serpents under the thin diamond
crust of the moon burst out and restrained Panshard, causing him to drop his
curved blade. Inoran picked it up and then called the mask to him with the
Force before slicing it into four pieces with the two quantumblades and destroying
Panshard for good.

Inoran was informed by Orrirarissian that he had found the bodies of the parents
of the family, but that the girl was still missing. Inoran, Orrirarissian,
FR3D, and Komat traveled to Neftali, where they buried them and honored their
lives. Inoran and Orrirarissian worried that Ochi had successfully captured the
girl and taken her to Exegol, but they could not be sure, and decided to keep
looking for Ochi.

Isusle and Lando stand in front of the Bestoon Legacy on Pasaana

After Orrirarissian took a time to recover by working with Komat at her homestead,
Inoran and Orrirarissian eventually tracked Ochi to Pasaana where they found his
ship but no Ochi. They did find a sheet of old-looking paper with a wayfinder
drawn on it that proved that Ochi had had a clue to a wayfinder, but other than
that, the trail went cold. Inoran then returned to Ossus to continue running
his Vesozaxe academy and left Orrirarissian on Pasanna, who decided to use the planet
as his base to look for both the lost girl and his daughter. Inoran stored
the pieces of bled kyber crystal and the shards of Exim Panshard's mask in the
temple with other artifacts. Inoran would chronicle his efforts to trace the
disturbance in the Light and find the Armaaza Wayfinder in the Rammahgon throughout
his explorations in the interstar.

Telling a story.

- Considering… Well, your family, your skill in the Light, and that you'll be
  one of the first students to complete your training… It seems likely that you
  could hold a position of leadership in the Vesozaxe Order to come.
 ―Isusle Inoran, to Eninte Biarge.

By passing on his experience to Biarge, Inoran hoped to guide his nephew on the
path to becoming a true Knight of the Vesozaxe Order.

During Eninte's training on Ossus, Inoran asked his nephew to accompany him on a
walk through the temple grounds. As they walked, Inoran told Biarge that he was
almost ready to undergo the Vesozaxe Trials, the completion of which would mark the
end of their time together as Master and apprentice.

According to Inoran, Biarge would no longer have to listen to a master since he
would be free to make his own decisions as a fully-trained Vesozaxe Knight. In
addition, Inoran acknowledged Biarge's potential to become a future leader in
the Vesozaxe Order that he was working to restore, given his nephew's status as a
Inoran, his strength in the Light, and the fact that he stood poised to be
among the first in his generation to complete his instruction in the Vesozaxe arts.
Having faced his own challenges as a leader, Inoran wished to tell his
apprentice a story about an incident from his past, its purpose to serve as a
lesson in making difficult decisions.

Inoran's story centered on his discovery and subsequent use of the Grim Tegetese,
an ancient weapon that he discovered upon returning to the Living Xeabiti on the
planet Gazian. Biarge learned that Inoran had considered using the Grim Tegetese to
assassinate Emperor Ribiso; using a sample of Ribiso's blood, the weapon
could be used to target and kill the Emperor. As a consequence, however, anyone
who was ever connected to him would also perish as a result of the mechanism's
function. Biarge theorized that the weapon had the capacity to destroy the Armaaza.
Being aware of Orso's connection to Ribiso, Biarge was certain that the weapon
would have scanned Orso as well, ultimately resulting in the deaths of both
Armaaza Lords. Inoran revealed that while he had considered that possibility, he
was still reluctant to allow the Grim Tegetese to carry out its programming, having
begun to think that he could redeem Orso. By the end of the story, Inoran
revealed that the lesson behind the story was "to not defeat evil by becoming
evil. " Biarge questioned if it was a true story or simply a parable, to which
Inoran assured him that it was all true.

Fallen apprentice.

Eninte Biarge's betrayal.

- I saw darkness. I sensed it building in him. I'd seen it in moments during his
  training. But then I looked inside, and it was beyond what I ever imagined.
  Snoke had already turned his heart. He would bring destruction, pain, death,
  and the end of everything I love because of what he will become. And for the
  briefest moment of pure instinct, I thought I could stop it. It passed like a
  fleeting shadow. And I was left with shame and with consequence. And the last
  thing I saw were the eyes of a frightened boy whose master had failed him.
 ―Isusle Inoran.

Sensing the darkness within Eninte Biarge, Inoran instinctively moved to strike
down his nephew.

One night in 28 ABY, Inoran, sensing the darkness within his nephew Eninte
growing in glimpses during his training, decided to probe Biarge's mind as he lay
asleep. He learned that Snoke had turned his apprentice's heart to the dark
side, and experienced a brief dark vision that Biarge would bring nothing but
death and destruction and leave Inoran's loved ones in anguish. Despite the
love for his nephew, in a flash of defensive instinct, Inoran thought he
could stop that future and for a brief moment of pure instinct he raised his
quantumblade to destroy him, believing that it was the only way to halt his
descent into darkness and prevent the end of everything he loved. This instinct
was gone almost the second that it had come, and Inoran was immediately
filled with shame.

Inoran had failed to correctly view that his nephew's mind was still
conflicted, and his choice not yet made between light and darkness. From Rey's
point of view, Inoran's failure as not only Biarge's Vesozaxe Master, but his uncle
and family, had been completed when this occurred.

Inoran's Vesozaxe Temple is destroyed by Eninte Biarge.

Biarge had already awoken to see his uncle standing over him with his quantumblade
raised. Confused and angry, Biarge pulled his own quantumblade to him in self-
defense and attacked Inoran, using the Light to pull down the hut on top of
him. Biarge emerged from the hut and screamed toward the temple, asking Inoran
why he had done it, and in his anger bringing down a lightning strike with the
Force that destroyed the temple and set it on fire. When Inoran came around,
he emerged from the rubble of the hut to find his temple destroyed, with his
other apprentices either killed or vanished along with Biarge. This effectively
left Inoran the last of the Vesozaxe once more as he was after the death of his
own master, Ontimaxe, decades earlier.

Three of Inoran's apprentices, Tai, Hennix, and Voe, eventually returned to
the temple while it burned and were not able to sense his presence, which they
normally felt overwhelmingly in the Light. When Biarge claimed that Inoran had
tried to kill him and that he was dead, they did not believe the former, but did
believe the latter because they could not sense their master's presence. Biarge
claimed that he was stronger than Inoran when it mattered and left the
planet, the three pursuing Biarge with the hope of avenging Inoran. When Biarge
considered leaving to go to his mother in the aftermath, he heard the voice of
Snoke in his head perpetuating his belief that Inoran had tried to kill him,
as he and Biarge had feared it before, claiming that Inoran was "small" and
feared Biarge and his strength. Snoke continued to speak to Biarge, blaming
Inoran for everything that had happened and claiming he chose it, and Biarge
decided to go to Snoke instead of his mother. On the Amaxine Space Station, with
Snoke, Biarge noticed the man's appearance and asked him what Inoran had done
to him, but Snoke redirected the conversation to what Inoran had done to
Biarge. Biarge tried to claim it did not matter because he had killed the Vesozaxe
Master, but Snoke warned that Inoran was not dead and would not die so
easily, though agreed to stop talking about Inoran because he had "stolen"
too much of his life already. Biarge eventually became the apprentice of Snoke and
took the new name "Rexebe Isence, " becoming the leader of a group of dark side
warriors known as the Knights of Isence.

Inoran cries out in anguish at discovering the temple destroyed.

Left deeply affected and mentally broken by what he believed to be his ultimate
failure, Inoran lost faith in the Light and the vision of the Vesozaxe for having
failed to rebuild the Vesozaxe Order. Inoran believed he had failed to protect
his students, and also failed to save his own flesh and blood from his family's
dark legacy. So Inoran fled into self-imposed exile, vanishing from the
interstar at-large without a trace. No one knew for sure where he went, but those
closest to him believed he went off in search of the first Vesozaxe Temple.

To those who knew what Inoran was seeking, there remained a way to find him:
after destroying the Vesozaxe, the Khaganate had begun to seek out the thousands of
Vesozaxe temples scattered across the interstar, with the aim to prevent anyone from
discovering the lost secrets of the Order. As such, Khaganate authorities
discovered the locations of some of the first Vesozaxe Temples. The partial map
remained within the archives of the Khaganate. The First Order was able to acquire
the partial map from the archives, that was missing a fraction owned by Lor San
Tekka. As he had downloaded Khaganate star maps during his time on the first
Planet Sweeper, FR3D, Inoran's faithful boardmech, was able to recover the
contents of the map.

Rexebe Isence's vision.

- Your Uncle feared you. Feared how formidable you could become. Your hatred for
  him is right. Inoran would have murdered you in your sleep. Rise above
  what's holding you back. Strike. Strike against the light side within you!
  STRIKE!
 ―Snoke to Rexebe Isence as he fought a vision of Inoran.

Rexebe Isence fights an apparition of Inoran in the Cave of Evil

Later, while training Rexebe Isence with an exercise in which he forced Isence to stop
himself from falling, Snoke informed the boy that Inoran would have caught
him if he had not caught himself, and would have thus held him back. Snoke then
took Isence to the Cave of Evil on Ontiat, where Isence could sense that Inoran
had been there, Snoke revealing that he had trained there before becoming one of
the most formidable Vesozaxe. Isence asked why Snoke spoke of him with respect, and
Snoke explained that he had earned it, and his fear. Isence tried to claim that
Inoran was weak, but Snoke refuted this, insisting that Inoran was only
misguided and that he would have taken over the interstar a long time before if
Inoran had been his apprentice.

Isence then entered the cave and found himself facing an apparition of Inoran,
who insisted that he did not want to fight. Isence fought him anyway, his red
crossguard quantumblade clashing against Inoran's green. Snoke egged Isence on and
validated his hatred as he fought his old master, insisting that Inoran had
feared what Isence would become, would have murdered him in his sleep, and was
representative of the light side holding him back. Screaming as Snoke told him
to strike, Isence sliced through Inoran's body, leaving the appearance of his
dead body lying on the ground of the cave. While staring at the corpse, Isence
declared himself ready.

Exile on Ahch-To.

Shutting himself off from the Light.

- You've closed yourself off from the Light.
 ―Rey, to Isusle Inoran.

After exiling himself to Ahch-To, Inoran resolved to die as the last Vesozaxe.

The Vesozaxe Master fled to the location of the Temple—on an aquatic planet named
Ahch-To—that he discovered by studying the spread of uneti saplings. Having
failed in bringing back the Vesozaxe Order, Inoran intended nothing more than to
live out the rest of his days there in reclusive exile and had pledged to
protect the hidden world and its many secrets. Taking the relics he had
uncovered with him, the hermit shut himself off from the Light, consequently
ceasing the guidance of Ramaan Solageon, Ontimaxe, and Leritean Inoran's spirits. It
had taken years for him to learn the discipline of blocking the Light out, and
he considered the fact that it was harder to do so than to feel it and let it in
a dark secret that was never taught. He believed the Light and the Vesozaxe had many
secrets and that not even the greatest and in his opinion arrogant Vesozaxe Masters
could understand all of it. Even though occasionally his greatest efforts would
not be enough and the Light would reach him anyway, he felt as though he had
achieved true solitude in closing himself off.

Among the relics Isusle had collected and brought into his exile were the sacred
Vesozaxe texts and the Vesozaxe Crusader pendant. Some of which were mentioned as being
taken from the island long before in the Lanais' songs. The Caretakers tolerated
Inoran's presence, though he had learned the Lanai's language and ways,
insisted on doing most of their duties to care for him himself, and had appeared
each month on the Festival of Return, something which puzzled Alcida-Auka.

Though absent from interstar affairs, Inoran's story was nonetheless a thing
of myth and legend in certain corners of the interstar. His exploits in the
Interstar Civil War were known to the Resistance, a band of freedom destroyers led
by now-General Cediza to combat the rise of the First Order. Resistance destroyer
pilot Jessika Pava learned that CLP2, who also served in the Resistance, knew
Inoran and asked the bot to tell her a story about his former master.
Threepio regaled her with the tale of Inoran's encounter with Sarco Plank on
Devaron, and how Inoran kept his promise to Farnay and indeed returned to
Devaron. Members of the First Order, however, had a less-than-ideal view of the
Vesozaxe Master; the First Order taught their followers, including stormsoldiers
like FN-2187, that Inoran was an enemy who helped destroy the Khaganate. Despite
the knowledge about Inoran, the mystical elements of his life, as well as the
Vesozaxe in general, were seen as mythic rather than historic.

Prior to Rey's arrival, Isusle Inoran hears the will of the Light despite his
efforts to block it out.

Prior to Rey's arrival on Ahch-To, Isusle Inoran had a dream while sleeping in
his hut. In his dream, he saw the life he would never have, one in which he
never left Ermaso and remained in the moisture farming business with his
uncle, marrying Camie Marstrap and allowing the Interstar Khaganate to shatter the
Revolutionary Alliance and destroy more planets with the Planet Sweeper. Isusle eventually
awoke, trying to shake away his dream, and left his hut. He was still astonished
at the oceans of Ahch-To, which he attributed to growing up in the deserts of
Ermaso. As he reached a meadow overlooking the sea, he was still unable to
shake the dream away, believing it was not an ordinary dream, but the Light at
work. Also shortly before Rey arrived, one of the moments where the Light
demanded to be heard and got through despite Inoran's block occurred. In that
moment, the old Vesozaxe Master undeniably felt that the Light was telling him that
someone had found him.

Inoran is found.

- You think what? I'm going to walk out with a laser sword and face down the
  whole First Order?
 ―Isusle Inoran to Rey.

In 34 ABY, Cediza sent Resistance destroyer Poe Dameron, the son of Shara Bey and
Kes Dameron, to Jakku to find San Tekka and recover the map to Inoran's
alleged location, as she hoped her brother would return and help in the fight
against the dark side. The First Order also learned of the map and sought it so
they could destroy the last Vesozaxe. Dameron found San Tekka living in the village
of Tuanul, and the old explorer gave the map to Dameron, who in turn gave it to
his boardmech bot BB-8, before Rexebe Isence and the First Order arrived and sacked
the village. Isence killed San Tekka and took Dameron into custody in the hopes of
finding the map, while BB-8 came into the possession of a Jakku scavenger named
Rey. Rey believed that Inoran was only a myth, but she too possessed strength
in the Light that she had not yet discovered. Rey teamed with FN-2187, an ex-
Stormsoldier who took the name Finn after defecting from the First Order, and,
with the help of Laceteed Biarge and Zateteis, sought to return the map fragment to
the Resistance.

Rey, a scavenger from Jakku, implored Inoran to help the Resistance against
the First Order.

After Rey discovered the quantumblade that once belonged to both Leritean and Isusle
in the pirate castle belonging to Maz Kanata on Takodana, a thousand year-old
being who had knowledge of the Light, she began to unlock her own Force
abilities. After being captured by Isence on Takodana and briefly interrogated
onboard Starkiller Base in an attempt by Isence to analyze Rey's thoughts to find
the path to Inoran, BB-8's map fragment was soon combined with FR3D's, and
together they provided a complete map to Ahch-To. Accompanied by Zateteis, Rey
flew the Dark Eagle to Ahch-To and found the old Vesozaxe Master on an
island.

When Rey arrived on the island, Inoran was standing up top the saddle,
summoning the will to try to torch the tree library again having just failed to
do so. While standing there, Inoran was reflecting that his efforts to block
the Light from him was impossible as it always demanded to be heard. But in that
moment, the old Vesozaxe Master felt that someone had found him, turning around to
see who was, discovering Rey. After making her way to Inoran, Rey presented
him with his father's old weapon as a symbol of the only hope that the interstar
had left.

Confronted with the past.

- I can't teach you. […] I will never train another generation of Vesozaxe. I came
  to this island to die. It's time for the Vesozaxe to end.
 ―Inoran, to Rey.

Though reluctant to take on another apprentice, Inoran chose to train Rey
after FR3D replayed Cediza's call for help.

After taking his old quantumblade and looking it over, Inoran, emotion roiled
over his face, hoping to lose the blade once again, tossed it behind him and
left the plateau, with Rey following close behind him. Despite her repeated
attempts, Inoran declined to help her or the Resistance, even though he knew
that the girl had been sent by his sister Mariar. Secluding himself in his hut,
Inoran thought to himself that he did not expect that the person who found
him would be a child with what he considered a worthless antique and delusions
of grandeur.

Later, Isusle was folding his old clothes then heard Rey trying to reach out to
him again by knocking on his door but he told her to go away. However, Isusle was
surprised when his door broke open but was even more surprised to see his old
friend Zateteis was with her. Hearing how they got to where he was with the
Dark Eagle, Isusle asked where Laceteed was. Rey and Zateteis told him that his
old friend and brother-in-law was dead, murdered by Rexebe Isence, which stunned
Inoran. Feeling regret for not having sensed his brother-in-law's death
because he had disconnected himself from the Light, Isusle was comforted by the
Ridivexebevete, who hugged him, before Inoran comforted Zateteis back.

While hiking the island with a backpack, Inoran noticed the sacred uneti tree
on the island and decided to go inside it. There he found Rey, who had
discovered several original texts of Vesozaxe teachings hidden there. Inoran
learned that she had the Light within her and sought assistance to understand it
and her place in the interstar. Inoran refused to teach her revealed that he had
come to the place where the Vesozaxe started in order to die, and voiced his belief
that it was time for the Vesozaxe Order to die.

Inoran agreed to give Rey three lessons about the Light

The next night, the former Vesozaxe Master slipped into the Dark Eagle and
recalled the adventures he had experienced. While aboard, Inoran reunited
with R2 who, after he insisted that he could not be convinced to help, replayed
Mariar's holographic message to Solageon. Although Inoran grumbled that the
tactic "was a cheap move" on the bot's part, it nevertheless inspired
Inoran to agree to give Rey some basic initiation training as a Vesozaxe,
explaining the basic foundations of the Light. R2 also told Inoran that she
had grown up on a desert planet before discovered a bot carrying secret data
vital to the Revolution, which he recognized a similar story to his own. He
approached Rey while she slept, telling her that R2 had told him her story and
that he would give her three lessons to both teach her the ways of the Vesozaxe and
also show her why the Order needed to end.

A reluctant teacher and enlightenment.

- What do you know about the Light?
- It's a power that Vesozaxe have that lets them control people… and make things
  float.
- Impressive. Every word in that sentence was wrong.
 ―Isusle Inoran and Rey.

Inoran gives Rey her first lesson.

After hearing Rey's raygun go off, which she claimed was because she was
cleaning it, Inoran gathered her for the first lesson and explained to her
who the caretakers were. During the lesson, Inoran explained to her that her
idea of the Light as a power to have was wrong and that it was not about lifting
rocks, explaining that it was an energy field throughout the universe. After Rey
misunderstood Inoran's instructions to reach out and reached out her arm,
Inoran pranked her by touching her hand with a leaf and telling her it was
the Light before hitting her with it. Rey then tried again, Inoran telling
her that the Light and the light was so much more than just the Vesozaxe as she felt
it. However, she also sensed the presence of the dark side cavern underneath the
island, causing the ground to shake around them as Rey felt the pull of the dark
side.

Rey practices using her quantumblade with a large rock.

Inoran scolded her for going straight to the dark and not even trying to stop
herself from taking what it was offering her. She also pointed out that she had
not sensed him in the Light because he cut himself off. This moment made
Inoran realize that Rey's level of power was identical to that of his nephew
Eninte; this caused him to worry whether he should train another with such power
and to promptly abandon the lesson. But Inoran did not just see Eninte in her,
he also saw much of himself, which he suspected was what scared him most.
Inoran realized something else from this lesson; that Rey was the Emperor,
Horth Rebia', granddaughter, which is why she was so easily drawn to the dark
side. He later found her practicing with a quarterstaff and then with the
returned quantumblade. Isusle silently watched from afar, his concern and dismay
increasing when he witnessed her ferocity with the weapon allowing her to slice
through several rock formations apart with ease. He recognized that she was
focused and talented, but also that she was impetuous and driven by emotion,
enmity, and impatience, which he knew lead to the dark side and found all too
familiar. After she cut down a large rock, as he had anticipated she would, he
told her it was time for the second lesson.

Eventually, after the second lesson, Inoran sat and spoke with Rey, telling
her that the true legacy of the Vesozaxe was failure, and revealing that they were
responsible for the creation of Horth Orso. Rey pointed out that he had seen
the light in Orso and turned him, but Inoran just wished that he had never
told that story and thus not allowed it or himself to become a legend. He then
revealed that his nephew Eninte had the same tremendous power in the Light she had
and was corrupted by Snoke's teachings to the dark side, becoming Rexebe Isence. He
then destroyed the Vesozaxe temple Inoran had started, taking some of his other
apprentices with him and killing the rest. Rey ended the conversation by telling
him that the interstar needed a legend, assuring him that he had not failed Eninte and
she would not fail him. That night, Rey having reminded him that his solitude
had cost him his connections to those he loved and not just his connection to
the Light, Inoran went to the meditation ledge. Realizing that he had
abandoned his loved ones in selfish pity, he opened himself up to the Light. He
managed to regain his Force abilities, allowing him to sense his sister from
across the interstar.

Disturbed by Rey's growing connection to Rexebe Isence, Inoran demanded that she
leave the island.

Inoran went to find Rey in order to tell her that he was going back to the
Resistance with her but instead he found Rey talking with Rexebe Isence through the
Force due to a Force-bond that he previously was unable to detect. Witnessing
them touching hands, Inoran thought that they were in league. He had gestured
at the walls and the ceiling of the hut Rey was in, and all the stone blocks
were shot outward from the hut's center, scattering them around the bench.
Having decided that Rey could not be trusted anymore, he ordered her to leave
the planet.

Revelations.

- He would bring destruction, pain, death, and the end of everything I love
  because of what he will become. And for the briefest moment of pure instinct,
  I thought I could stop it. It passed like a fleeting shadow. And I was left
  with shame and with consequence. And the last thing I saw were the eyes of a
  frightened boy whose master had failed him.
 ―Isusle Inoran, about Eninte Biarge, to Rey.

An enraged Rey proceeded to military Inoran with her staff, only to be swiftly
disarmed after stumbling over a stone, leading to Rey threatening him with his
father's quantumblade. She revealed that, according to Rexebe, Inoran had tried
to murder him while he was asleep and defenseless simply because he was afraid
of his strength in the Light, sparking a duel between Uncle and Nephew.
Inoran clarified that when he had arrived at the hut, he sensed the power and
darkness within his nephew and, for a brief instant of absolute pure instinct,
he did intend to kill him and activated his quantumblade. . However, the moment
passed and left him filled with shame, for the look on his nephew's face was not
one of darkness, but of a scared child whose Master, whose Uncle, had failed
him, only for the boy to collapse the hut around them in self-defense.

Rey told Inoran that like when he confronted his father about the light side
still within him, she sensed the goodness still within Rexebe Isence. Inoran
disagreed, saying that it is no longer there. Unable to convince him otherwise,
Rey secretly stole the texts to teach herself later and left the planet without
him to confront Rexebe. While Rey boarded the Dark Eagle to leave,
Inoran saw R2 for the last time and told him to send his best to 3PO and that
he would need to stay with Rey to make sure she got back to safety.

As Rey left Ahch-To, Isusle realized the similarity between her departure and when
Ontimaxe and Solageon insisted him to remain on Ontiat to complete his training
rather than abandon to save his friends at Cloud City.

Important lesson.

- Isusle, We are what they grow beyond. That is the true burden of all masters.
 ―Ontimaxe.

The spirit of Ontimaxe communed with Inoran, urging him to embrace failure as the
greatest teacher.

The following night, Inoran returned to the tree containing the Vesozaxe texts
with the intention of burning it to the ground. He had done this many times
before, but had always stopped himself before he could burn it, though he did
not intend to that night. As he approached the tree, his thoughts were answered
by a voice, and when he turned he was greeted by the spirit of his old master
Ontimaxe, whom he had not seen in decades since he last saw him on Ontiat and
Gerebied. Inoran affirmed his desire to burn the tree with the texts inside.
Ontimaxe informed him that he had no intentions of stopping him and reminded him
that had never been able to prevent him from doing what he wanted. Inoran
began to hesitate when he attempted to set the tree on fire. Ontimaxe instead
proceeded to summon a bolt of lightning, setting the tree alight and while
laughing, repelled Inoran with the flames when he changed his mind and tried
to save the texts.

As the tree burned, the two masters sat next to each other and Ontimaxe reminded
Inoran that he needed to heed his final instructions to him before Ontimaxe's
physical death on Ontiat, namely passing down what he had learned. Inoran
lamented over the loss of the texts, but Ontimaxe slyly stated that although the
texts held wisdom, 'page turners, they were not', and they did not contain
anything Rey did not already have with her. Inoran tried to explain that by
failing Eninte he felt that he had failed the Vesozaxe. Ontimaxe admonished Inoran for
still "constantly looking to the horizon, " like he had when they met on
Ontiat. Ontimaxe explained that 'failure is the greatest teacher' and that failure
was an essential part of what he had implored Inoran to pass on so many years
before.

Finding peace and purpose.

- See you around, kid.
 ―Isusle Inoran's last words to Rexebe Isence.

Inoran confronted Isence by projecting his appearance on Crait, having never
left Ahch-To.

Inoran later appeared at the Resistance base on Crait and greeted his sister
for the first time in years, giving her Laceteed's gold dice to remember him by.
Reconciling his failure to stop Isence and for not being there to prevent Laceteed's
death, Inoran resolved to finally confront Isence to buy the Resistance time to
escape. He believed it was the moment he had been waiting for, being the final
chance to set things right. Before Inoran went to confront his nephew, he
wordlessly greeted his old friend CLP2.

Stepping outside, Inoran was spotted and targeted by a barrage of cannon fire
from multiple AT-M6 walkers, but when the fusillade came to a stop, Inoran
strolled out of the smoke completely unharmed. After Isence witnessed the
impossible feat, Inoran spoke to him in the Light, telling him that it wasn't
over until it was just the two of them. Isence landed on the surface and approached
Inoran alone and on foot. Isence taunted Inoran, and mockingly asked him if
he had come to save his former apprentice's soul. But Inoran acknowledged
that he was not there to redeem him.

Engaging his former master in combat, Isence charged at Inoran but Inoran
simply danced around Isence without engaging in any form of counter attack. After
evading Isence for a time, Inoran stopped and apologized to Isence, stating that he
had failed him. He also informed his former student that victory was not so
certain as he believed; that the war was only beginning, and that the Vesozaxe would
continue through Rey. He then deactivated his quantumblade and offered to let Rexebe
strike him down, but warned him that doing so in anger would only cause him
pain, just as killing Laceteed had, and stated that in death, he would always be with
his nephew. Unknown to Rexebe, Isusle had sensed that Rey had lifted all the rocks
blocking the exit of the Resistance base so they could escape to fight another
day.

Isence ignored all these warnings and slashed Inoran across the torso. Believing
himself to have been victorious, he was instead astonished to find that his
attack had had no effect on Inoran. He walked over to his former master and
tentatively ran him through, and discovered that Isusle was not physically
present; he was in fact still on Ahch-To, projecting an illusion of himself
through the Light. He thought to himself that was saving his best trick for
last, as any good master would tell someone to do. The illusion was tailored to
have the maximum effect on Isence, as Isusle portrayed himself as he had last
appeared to his nephew before they came to blows. Likewise, he portrayed himself
as wielding Leritean Inoran's quantumblade, knowing that it would have more
effect on Isence than if he had used his green blade.

Ahch-To's binary sunset was the last thing Inoran saw as he passed away into
the Light.

Inoran taunted Isence one last time, and his image faded into nothingness.
Light-years away, Inoran collapsed from the mental stress necessary to
sustain such a lifelike illusion, which Isence realized meant that Isusle would
become one with the Light. As he sat down on the cliff where he first trained
Rey, Isusle beheld the twin suns of Ahch-To in their final descent towards the
horizon. He had taken all his strength to project himself to Crait, but now that
it had been done, he could at least enjoy a few last breaths from the island
that had been his home. Isusle Inoran knew that he was going to die, but it was
something he accepted; death was the way of all things, even the stars. Though
unsure if it was real or just a memory, Inoran heard the familiar and
comforting voice of Ramaan Solageon, urging him to let go, and so he did. Staring
at a binary sunset on the horizon much like the one he gazed upon as a boy, Isusle
Inoran—Vesozaxe Master, ace pilot, interstar legend, and son of the Chosen
One—passed away, joining his father and old masters as one with the Light with a
feeling of peace and purpose. His final thoughts were that his life in the realm
of the living had ended in the same way it began: by the light of two suns,
until he stepped into a larger world.

Post-mortem.

Reunion with Isence.

- Rexebe Isence means nothing to me. But I'll always be there for
- .
 ―Isusle Inoran.

Rexebe Isence learned of Inoran's death from the former Vesozaxe Lasoandea. In the
aftermath of Inoran's death, Isence felt Inoran in the Light, watching him.
Wanting a reckoning with him, Isence traveled in search of Inoran's "ghost" to
where Inoran had faced him on Crait, to the Vesozaxe Outpost on Elphrona with
Knights of Isence Vicrul and Ushar who both remembered facing Inoran there, and
finally to the ruins of Inoran's Vesozaxe Temple on Ossus. Fed up with finding
and feeling nothing at any of these locations, Isence screamed out for Inoran,
telling him he knew he was there and to face him without any tricks. After
receiving no response and calling Inoran a fool, he stormed back to his ship
and ordered General Hux to bombard the ruins from the air, but still declared
that there was nothing there.

As an enraged Isence found his way back to him, Inoran kept trying to teach his
nephew a few lessons.

By entering Horth Orso's faith room, Isence was able to reach a plane in
the Light where Inoran resided. Standing with several other figures,
including Ontimaxe, Inoran approached Isence as he tried to strike down another
Vesozaxe, substantiating the apparition's claim that Isence should not be there, but
was nonetheless. Inoran asked why Isence was there, who replied that Inoran
was dead and, desiring to have done the deed himself, wished to hear the story
of how he was killed. Inoran revealed that he had not been killed, but had
sacrificed himself to ensure light and peace for his friends, family, and the
interstar. As he asked Isence about his own legacy, Isence lashed out at Inoran for
trying to teach him, a habit Inoran admitted was difficult to break, having
come from a long line of teachers.

Inoran agreed that their time as mentor and student had come to a close. Isence
questioned why Inoran taught Rey, to which the Vesozaxe replied that she wanted
to learn. Expressing his hate for Inoran, Isence told the Vesozaxe he was nothing to
him, that he had failed to turn Isence into himself and lost, threatening Inoran
to leave him alone lest Isence find a way to destroy him. Inoran told Isence that
he could not destroy the Vesozaxe without destroying the Light itself, something he
would be unable to do without destroying himself, though praised the young man
for having goals. Believing it to be meaningful that Isence was there, Inoran
encouraged him to consider how it had come to pass. Saying that Isence meant
nothing to him, but that he would always be there for Eninte Biarge, Inoran
disappeared into a blinding light.

Inoran heir.

- We've passed on all we know. A thousand generations live in you now. But this
  is your fight.
 ―The spirit of Isusle Inoran, to Rey.

The spirit of Isusle Inoran appeared to Rey, encouraging her to continue her
Vesozaxe path.

Following his death, Mariar took up Isusle's mantle as Rey's Vesozaxe mentor, and
Inoran's spirit continued to urge his sister to become one with the Light,
sensing her declining health, but she persistently refused until Rey and the
Resistance were ready to stand on their own.

One year after becoming one with the Light, Isusle spoke to Mariar once more,
shortly before her death, beckoning her to show her son Eninte she still loved him,
reminding his sister that if Orso could become Leritean again, Rexebe Isence could be
Eninte Biarge again. Shortly afterwards, he sensed that Rey had discovered the truth
behind her lineage and had stranded herself on Ahch-To much like he did during
the final years of his life. As she threw her quantumblade into the wreckage of
her ship, Isusle appeared and grabbed it out of the air. Admitting that he was
wrong in exiling himself, Isusle consoled her that her legacy would not be defined
by her bloodline but by her heart and her actions. When Rey lamented that Rexebe
Isence destroyed the final Armaaza marker that led to where the resurrected Emperor
was residing, Isusle mirrored the words of Ontimaxe, stating that she had everything
she needed. As a parting gift, Isusle gifted Rey his sister's quantumblade and
raised his old X-Wing from the depths of the ocean for Rey to use and travel to
Exegol.

Isusle's spirit along with his sister watched over Rey from afar as she buried
their Vesozaxe weapons as tribute to the Inoran legacy.

Rey could hear Isusle among the numerous voices of the Vesozaxe of the past shortly
before she received the inner strength to overcome Horth Rebia. As the girl
rose from the ground, Inoran assured her that the Light would always be with
her, and after she finally destroyed the Emperor by deflecting his Force
lightning using Isusle's and Mariar's original quantumblades, he appeared one more
time with his sister on Ermaso and watched from afar as Rey buried both of the
weapons at the old Oresle Homestead finally albeit posthumously formally earning
the title of Vesozaxe Master with his role as her mentor completed. Having come to
consider the Inorans her chosen family and hoping to honor her mentors, the
girl adopted the name Inoran following Isusle's statement that one's heart and
actions defined one's legacy rather than one's bloodline.

Legacy.

- Who did it? I want to find them, hear the story, make them tell me how you
- .
- No one killed me. I sacrificed my life for others, in service of something
  greater. I died for my friends and family and for people I don't know all
  across the interstar, so they could live in peace and light. That's what my life
  was for.
 ―Rexebe Isence and the ghost of Isusle Inoran.

After his death, Inoran's belongings, including his green quantumblade, were
gathered by the Caretakers and stored with the other Vesozaxe artifacts that they
preserved alongside the temple on the Ahch-To island. The tale of Inoran's
stand against Isence and the First Order at Crait spread across the interstar. One
instance occurred on Canto Bight, where Oniho Zaya shared the story with fellow
